a:T45a,The approach is continued-BPE tokenizer expansion. Starting from the 65K byte-level BPE tokenizer used by LFM2, the authors keep the existing merges fixed and append new merges learned on a multilingual corpus weighted toward under-tokenized languages, while retaining English and code to avoid losing compression on existing workloads. The expanded tokenizer has a 128K vocabulary. This construction gives two useful mapping properties: most old tokens survive as single tokens in the new vocabulary, and every newly added token has an exact decomposition into a sequence of old tokens. The model initialization follows this structure. Rows corresponding to carried-over tokens are copied directly. Rows for new tokens are initialized as the mean of the embeddings of their old-token decomposition. Adaptation then proceeds in two stages: first, train only the embedding-related parameters while freezing the model body; second, run full-model continued pretraining on a balanced multilingual mixture. After this tokenizer adaptation, the model enters the usual mid-training and supervised post-training pipeline.b:T44c,The recipe is applied to an LFM2-8B-A1B checkpoint, described as an 8B-parameter on-device MoE model. The vocabulary is expanded from roughly 65K to 128K. Out of 64,400 source tokens, 63,151 remain single tokens in the expanded vocabulary, enabling direct row copying. The tokenizer compression gains are large for the target languages: about 2.4x fewer tokens for Hindi, 2.6x fewer for Vietnamese, and up to 4.0x fewer for Thai on the same text. After accounting for the higher per-token cost of the larger vocabulary, the authors estimate a 2.2–3.7x per-character decode speedup on the reference devices for these languages. The provided excerpt states that Section 6 shows no sacrifice of accumulated quality and that Section 8 reports negative findings, but it does not include the detailed benchmark tables, training-token counts, or ablation numbers. The most important experimental questions to check in the full paper are the necessity of embedding-only adaptation, the robustness of mean initialization, old-language regression, and whether the latency gains survive on real edge hardware.c:T482,Priority sections for close reading: Section 2 (third-party content injection threat model), focusing on the attack surface definition, differences from traditional poisoning vectors, and why public comments are a viable vector. Section 3 (HalfLife analysis method), the core technical contribution, with attention to the per-stage retention rate calculation logic and how to reproduce measurements using publicly available filtering tools. Section 4 (comparative experiments on two injection vectors), reviewing retention rate data for public comments vs. programmatic ads and the underlying reasons for differences in filter evasion ability. Section 5 (scaling experiments on poisoning effect), focusing on the relationship between poison proportion and behavior induction success rate, and how much poisoning effect survives instruction tuning. Points worth further exploration: reuse of the HalfLife framework to assess risks from other potential poisoning vectors (e.g., Wikipedia talk pages, open-source repo issue sections, forum posts); blind spots in current quality filtering rules for detecting malicious content formatted as natural comments.d:T4bc,CIPHER is implemented as a directed acyclic execution graph on top of LangGraph, taking a dataset plus a textual user goal as input and producing a textual analysis result as output. Its core innovation is the DES framework, split into two independent stages: 1) Exploration: a Planner Agent generates N candidate initial plans (N is a hyperparameter), aiming to cover a diverse set of solution paths; 2) Selection: an independent selection strategy picks M out of N candidates (M < N, M is a hyperparameter) for subsequent execution, concentrating compute on high-value initial states. The M selected states run in parallel to reduce latency. Each execution branch consists of a Feature Agent (proposing feature engineering), an iterative code refinement loop (Coder generates draft → execution → Analyzer jointly diagnoses logs and code → Editor makes local edits, repeated until iteration budget b is exhausted), and a Finalizer Agent (summarizing execution outcomes). Finally, an Aggregator Agent synthesizes outputs from all parallel branches into the final answer. The code loop uses local edits instead of full regeneration, preserving error-correction capability while reducing generation overhead.e:T8d0,实验分成四块，结构比较完整。

第一块是合成任务 S5-Retrieval。这个任务同时要求不可解群状态跟踪和 in-context retrieval，单纯 Transformer 或单纯 recurrent 模型都难以胜任。作者用它证明中层时间递归确实能表达“跨步持续的抽象状态”，不是只在自然语言任务上靠数据偏置取巧。摘要里没有给出具体数值，但正文结论是 shallow T^2MLR 能解这类标准架构失败的组合任务，这对机制层面的说服力很重要。

第二块是自然语言预训练。作者在 data- and parameter-matched 设置下比较 T^2MLR 与标准 Transformer，结果是 perplexity 更低，且下游 NLP benchmark 也更好。一个很关键的消融是：最好的收益出现在只对约 20% 的中间层做 recurrence/looping，而不是全层递归。这说明收益并不来自“多加一条状态通路”本身，而是来自把递归放在合适的层段。论文还把规模扩到 361M 和 1B 参数、50B pretraining tokens，收益仍然存在，说明不是小模型特有现象。

第三块是下游推理微调。作者在 GSM-Aug、ProsQA、HotPotQA、Variable Assignment 上都报告了优于参数和数据匹配基线的结果。这里最有信息量的观察不是单个 benchmark 提升多少，而是 middle-layer recurrence 变体持续优于 full-layer recurrence。也就是说，论文的核心架构判断在多个任务上都被重复验证了。

第四块是 retrofit 实验，这也是最接近实际采用场景的一部分。作者把递归路径插入到预训练好的 SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct，再做短暂数学数据微调。结果 GSM8K 从 35.8 提升到 39.9，MATH500 从 12.8 提升到 18.0，且对照组是“同样微调但没有递归改造”的基线。这个结果说明 T^2MLR 不一定要求从头预训练，至少在已有模型上加一条中层递归支路也能带来可见收益。

成本方面，论文给出的推理额外开销最多约 8% per-token，这一点很关键。因为很多 latent reasoning 或 depth recurrence 方法的主要问题不是无效，而是推理成本太高，难以进入真实 serving。T^2MLR 的 trade-off 是把更多复杂性放在训练端，换取较低的在线开销。f:T9f0,The core idea is temporal middle-layer recurrence. The model caches a representation from a deeper middle layer at token t-1 and injects it into an earlier middle layer at token t through a gated fusion pathway. This creates a continuous latent channel across decoding steps, but places that channel inside the part of the network where abstract computation is believed to happen.

Two design choices matter. First, the recurrent signal does not re-enter through the embedding layer. It is fused directly into the middle of the computation graph, so the previous token’s abstract state can continue evolving in the current token’s middle-layer processing. Second, recurrence is localized to a subset of middle layers rather than applied everywhere. The rationale is functional: early layers are more lexical/syntactic, late layers are closer to vocabulary projection, and middle layers are the best place to preserve reasoning state.

Architecturally, T^2MLR defines a write location and a read location. A hidden state from the write layer is cached, then consumed at the read layer of the next token. A gating module controls how much of the current token’s local representation should be mixed with the carried-over latent state. This is more flexible than a plain residual addition because the model can decide contextually when to rely on persistent latent state and when to ignore it.

The training story is a major part of the contribution. Exact temporal recurrence would introduce token-to-token dependencies under teacher forcing and break standard sequence-parallel training. The paper proposes an approximate temporal-parallel training scheme that preserves scalable parallelism while still training the recurrent pathway. This is the practical enabler: without it, the architecture would behave more like an RNN-style serial model and would be hard to pretrain at scale. The approximation introduces a train-time compromise between recurrence fidelity and throughput, and that trade-off is central to evaluating the method.

Relative to prior work, the distinctions are clean. Compared with latent-recurrent methods, recurrence is placed in the middle layers rather than at the input side. Compared with looped/depth-recurrent Transformers, extra computation is propagated across time instead of repeatedly re-running the full stack for one token, so inference overhead stays small. Compared with a standard Transformer, the model adds an explicit continuous hidden-state pathway across tokens, reducing the token bottleneck.10:T84e,The empirical section is broad and well structured.

First, the paper uses the synthetic S5-Retrieval benchmark, which combines non-solvable group state tracking with in-context retrieval. The point is not benchmark coverage but mechanism stress-testing: standard Transformers and recurrent models fail in isolation, while a shallow T^2MLR can solve the task. Even without focusing on the exact number, this result supports the claim that middle-layer temporal recurrence can maintain abstract state across steps.

Second, in language pretraining, T^2MLR achieves lower perplexity than parameter- and data-matched Transformer baselines and also improves downstream NLP benchmarks. A particularly informative ablation is that the best gains come from applying recurrence to only about 20% of the middle layers rather than the full stack. That strongly suggests the benefit comes from placing recurrence in the right region, not from adding generic extra machinery. The paper also scales experiments to 361M and 1B parameters with 50B pretraining tokens, and the gains persist.

Third, in downstream reasoning finetuning, the model consistently beats matched baselines on GSM-Aug, ProsQA, HotPotQA, and Variable Assignment. The most important pattern is not any single benchmark delta, but the repeated finding that middle-layer recurrence outperforms full-layer recurrence. That reinforces the paper’s central architectural thesis.

Fourth, the retrofit experiment is practically important. The authors insert the recurrent pathway into a pretrained SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct model and do brief math finetuning. GSM8K improves from 35.8 to 39.9, and MATH500 from 12.8 to 18.0, compared with an identically finetuned baseline without the recurrent modification. This suggests the architecture may be adoptable without full pretraining from scratch.

On efficiency, the paper reports at most about 8% extra per-token inference overhead. That matters because many latent-reasoning or depth-looping methods fail the deployment test due to decoding cost. T^2MLR shifts more complexity to training while keeping online overhead modest.11:T6e5,Start with the problem framing in the introduction, then move quickly to the method section that argues why recurrence should live in the middle layers. The main value is not the gating formula by itself, but the combination of layer specialization and temporal recurrence.

Three parts deserve close reading. First, inspect the architecture figure and the exact recurrence/fusion definition: which layer writes the cache, which layer reads it, how the gate is parameterized, whether parameters are shared, what tensor is cached, and when it is updated. Those details determine whether this is truly a lightweight cross-token latent state or just another way of deepening the network.

Second, study the approximate temporal-parallel training scheme. This is the most important engineering contribution. Focus on how teacher-forced recurrence is approximated, where the approximation error comes from, whether it creates train-test mismatch, and how it interacts with sequence parallelism. If large-scale pretraining feasibility is your main concern, this section matters more than the benchmark tables.

Third, read the ablations carefully: middle-only versus full-layer recurrence, the fraction of layers used for recurrence, comparisons against looped Transformers and latent-recurrent baselines, and the inference overhead measurements. These experiments answer the two key design questions: why not recur everywhere, and why not just loop depth within a token.

If time is limited, the best path is: method figure, training approximation, main pretraining table, layer-placement ablations, and the retrofit experiment. A serious read is about 60–90 minutes; reproducing or adapting the method would require another pass over the training-parallelism details.12:T85f,The method models each MDLM denoising step as two actions. First, the model predicts tokens for all currently masked positions. Second, among those newly predicted positions, it chooses which ones remain revealed and which ones are remasked for later steps. This yields a two-stage action MDP. As a result, the trajectory probability factorizes into a token policy term and a masking-policy term, and the policy gradient decomposes accordingly: one term reinforces good token choices, the other reinforces good ordering decisions.

A practical strength is that the masking term does not require a separate policy head. The authors derive unmasking scores from the model’s existing logits and turn them into position log-probabilities via a softmax. So the method adds no extra parameters and no extra forward passes beyond those already needed for token likelihood computation. That is important for diffusion LMs, where generation chains are already longer than AR decoding.

The theoretical claim is that dropping the masking term can miss ascent directions for expected return. Intuitively, this makes sense: in an MDLM, deciding which positions to resolve early and which uncertain positions to defer changes the future conditional distributions and can affect final answer quality. Optimizing only token predictions leaves part of the policy frozen.

Another positive feature is compatibility. The paper does not introduce a bespoke RL framework; it plugs the decomposition into standard policy-gradient style algorithms. Any method that relies on log-probabilities over sampled trajectories can, in principle, incorporate both token and masking log-probs. That makes the proposal easier to adopt than an entirely new optimizer.

Compared with prior work, the differences are clear. Relative to ELBO-based RL approximations, it moves from a reconstruction view to an explicit trajectory-policy view. Relative to token-only trajectory methods, it treats unmasking order as part of the policy. Relative to approaches that would need an extra network for ordering, it reuses the model’s own logits and keeps the engineering overhead low.13:T678,The main empirical focus is mathematical reasoning and code generation. The headline result is consistent improvement from jointly optimizing the token and masking terms over methods that optimize only the token component. The abstract reports gains of up to +4.0% on MATH500, +2.9% on HumanEval, and +2.5% on GSM8K and MBPP. The paper also claims top-tier performance across these tasks, suggesting the benefit is not isolated to a single benchmark.

The most informative part should be the ablations. The critical comparisons are likely: token-gradient only, masking-gradient only, and both together; sensitivity to remasking schedules; and alternative constructions of the position log-probability. If the joint objective consistently wins, that supports the claim that the masking policy contributes genuine learning signal rather than acting as a noisy regularizer.

The paper should also be read against prior ELBO-based RL and token-only trajectory RL baselines. For those comparisons to be meaningful, one should check whether rollout count, denoising steps, reward normalization, and the underlying MDLM backbone are controlled. RL results for diffusion LMs can otherwise be confounded by sampling budget and decode schedule.

A practical point is efficiency. The authors emphasize that the masking term comes at no extra forward cost beyond token-likelihood computation. That means the gains are not simply purchased with more compute; they come from extracting more supervision from the same sampled trajectory. If the full paper includes wall-clock or sample-efficiency numbers, those would be especially useful for judging real adoption value.14:T5c9,Do not start from the result tables. Start from the formalization. The central idea is that one MDLM denoising step consists of two actions—token prediction and remasking—not that remasking is an implementation detail.

First, read the preliminaries that define the MDLM trajectory and sampling process. Make sure you understand the exact remasking semantics: whether the method only decides which newly predicted positions stay revealed, or whether previously revealed tokens can be changed again. That choice determines the MDP state and action space.

Second, read the two-stage MDP and policy-gradient derivation carefully. This is the core of the paper. Focus on how the trajectory probability factorizes, how the position log-probability is derived from logits, and how the theory section argues that ignoring the masking term can miss improving directions. If you only read one section, read this one.

Third, inspect the main comparisons and ablations. Prioritize token-only versus token-plus-masking, consistency of gains across tasks, and any signs of instability or reward hacking. For RL on diffusion LMs, variance and training stability often matter more than the best single score.

A quick pass should take 30–45 minutes. If you work on diffusion LMs or any-order language models, it is worth a second pass to rewrite the equations and pseudocode in your own notation, because this paper directly affects how log-probabilities and advantages should be defined.15:T481,On a 135M-class model trained on FineWeb-Edu (training mean recursion depth = 8): 1) Convergence properties: Mean successive-output KL drops from 3.9×10^-1 at loop 2 to 8.5×10^-6 at loop 16, with hidden state change decaying in lockstep; per-token convergence is highly heterogeneous — median token converges by loop 6, ~10% of tokens still update at the training-mean depth of 8, with convergence order correlated with token type. 2) Depth allocation performance: The training-free convergence exit policy matches depth-8 quality at 4.94 average loops (38% lower than fixed depth 8), and tracks fixed-depth quality exactly across the average-depth range; by contrast, a linear router trained on the same convergence labels requires nearly full depth with no compute savings, a result reproduced at two model scales. 3) Distribution alignment: A model trained with mean depth 4 saturates at depth 4, and a mean-depth-8 model saturates at depth 8, consistent with external experimental results from Huginn. 4) Depth elasticity: A single checkpoint covers depths 1-8 with monotonic quality gains, and extrapolates stably to 4x the training-mean depth.16:T8a4,The method, xHC (Expanded Hyper-Connections), builds on mHC and introduces two complementary changes.

The first is temporal feature augmentation, aimed at the information bottleneck. In HC/mHC, the multi-stream state is supposed to preserve different weighted histories, but each layer writes back only a relatively narrow signal. As N grows, the extra streams become increasingly redundant because there is not enough diverse information to populate them. xHC enriches the write-back signal with low-cost temporal features extracted along the causal token sequence using multi-scale causal convolutions. The point is not convolution for its own sake; it is a cheap way to inject broader local temporal context than a single-token layer output, giving the expanded streams more differentiated content to store.

The second is a sparse residual-stream architecture, aimed at the computational bottleneck. In mHC, a major cost comes from generating an input-dependent residual mixing matrix H_res in R^{N×N}. Since this predicts N^2 coefficients from an NC-dimensional state, the cost scales roughly as O(N^3 C). xHC keeps the read path dense so every layer can still access the full N-stream state, but activates only k out of N streams for residual mixing and write-back. This preserves the benefit of a large readable state while sparsifying the most expensive update path, reducing both FLOPs and memory traffic at large N.

These two components are structurally decoupled but synergistic. As N increases, temporal augmentation makes the extra streams more useful, while sparse updates keep them affordable. The method is therefore not just 'mHC plus sparsity' or 'mHC plus convolution'; it is a paired fix for the two reasons large-N expansion previously failed.

Architecturally, xHC is still a modification of the residual pathway on top of a Transformer/MoE backbone, not a replacement for attention or FFN. It is best viewed as adding a new axis for persistent state or memory capacity. Compared with MoE, which expands conditional compute capacity, xHC expands cross-layer carried state. Compared with simply increasing hidden width, it uses structured multi-stream state rather than one larger vector.17:T6b6,Two result groups matter most. First, at the 18B MoE scale, xHC reaches a final training loss of 1.758, compared with 1.776 for mHC and 1.799 for the vanilla residual baseline. It improves the average downstream score from 44.8 with mHC to 48.8, while adding only 4.1% training FLOPs relative to the vanilla baseline. That is important because the gain is not bought by a large compute increase.

Second, the expansion-rate scaling result directly supports the paper’s main claim. On a 2.5B MoE model, increasing N from 4 to 16 in mHC improves loss by only 0.006 while increasing training FLOPs by 32%. Under xHC, the same expansion from N=4 to N=16 improves loss by 0.012 with only 4% extra FLOPs. This shows that xHC changes the economics of scaling N, not just the point performance at one fixed setting.

The paper also includes scaling-law style compute-efficiency analysis. To reach the same loss, the vanilla baseline needs 1.50× the compute of xHC, and mHC needs 1.19×. That is more convincing than a single benchmark number because it suggests the gain is systematic across scales. Another useful result is optimizer robustness: xHC remains effective when trained with Muon, so the benefit does not appear tied to AdamW-specific optimization dynamics.

The most important ablations to verify while reading are the separate contributions of temporal augmentation and sparse residual updates, plus their combined effect. That determines whether xHC is a coherent design or just an additive bundle of tricks. If the full paper includes memory-traffic or activation-overhead analysis, that is also worth attention, since large-N architectures often hit bandwidth limits before raw FLOPs become the main issue.18:T78d,The method has two main components. First, Polestar-Cache uses token-level representational drift to decide where KV-cache entries have become stale and should be refreshed. Instead of assuming that tokens outside the current block remain valid, or relying on coarse proxies such as attention concentration or uncertainty, it directly tracks hidden-state changes across steps and layers. The motivation is specific to dLLMs: even if a token is not explicitly updated at the current step, its representation may still change substantially because neighboring tokens are being denoised under bidirectional attention. Polestar-Cache therefore performs sparse and sporadic KV refreshes only at high-drift positions, while reusing the rest.

Second, Polestar-Commit uses drift as a convergence signal for token commitment. The paper reports that token drift often exhibits a sharp spike near the onset of convergence toward the final output representation, followed by stabilization. This is used to decide when a token can be committed earlier and more safely. Compared with confidence-only policies, drift is better aligned with whether the representation is still being materially rewritten by context, which matters more in iterative bidirectional generation.

Third, the paper includes system-level optimization. Since measuring drift and scheduling refreshes introduce overhead, the implementation uses CPU offloading and latency hiding to overlap Polestar-specific operations with the main forward pass. This matters because many inference-time ideas look good algorithmically but lose their gains once control overhead is included.

Relative to prior work, Fast-dLLM relies on block-wise cache reuse and static commitment thresholds; Elastic-Cache and d2Cache use attention- or uncertainty-based proxies. Polestar’s distinguishing move is to use drift as a unified first-order signal for both cache refresh and token commitment.19:T51d,The evaluation spans multiple diffusion LLM families and reasoning-heavy domains such as math and coding. The headline numbers reported are up to 10.73% accuracy gain over prior baselines, 3.74% on average, up to 3.7× higher throughput, 1.6× on average, and decoding parallelism reaching 3.67 tokens per forward. The paper emphasizes improvement on the accuracy–throughput Pareto frontier rather than a single metric.

The more important experimental story is the validation chain behind those numbers. The paper aims to show: first, that drift is a useful indicator of cache staleness; second, that drift spikes correlate with token convergence; and third, that using drift for both cache refresh and commitment outperforms applying either idea in isolation. The key ablations are therefore likely to be: Polestar-Cache only, Polestar-Commit only, both together, different drift metrics or thresholds, and with versus without the system overlap optimizations.

When reading the full results, the most useful checks are whether gains hold across model families, whether longer generations benefit more, and whether quality degradation under more aggressive parallel commitment is meaningfully reduced. Those details determine whether drift is a robust control signal or a configuration-specific heuristic.1a:T451,Start with the introduction and the first two figures. They frame the real issue well: dLLM inference is slow not only because denoising takes multiple steps, but because bidirectional attention breaks the usual cache reuse assumptions. Then go directly to the section that defines and measures representational drift. That is the conceptual center of the paper, and it is where you can judge whether the method is grounded in model dynamics or just another heuristic.

After that, read the cache and commitment sections together. One answers where stale KV entries should be refreshed; the other answers when tokens can be safely committed. If time is limited, you can skim some formula details, but pay attention to how drift is aggregated across layers, steps, and tokens into actual control decisions. Finish with the Pareto plots and ablation tables to see whether the gains really come from the unified signal rather than extra tuning.

A useful skim should take around 30–45 minutes. If you work on diffusion or masked LMs, the appendix material on the prevalence of drift is worth a closer look.1b:T763,The method, D-cut, is a training-free adaptive depth pruning layer on top of speculative decoding. It has two main components.

First, cross-request pruning removes the uniform-depth constraint. The authors observe large variance in accepted lengths across requests and show that drafter confidence is informative about whether a token will be accepted. Instead of verifying depth D for every request, D-cut treats all draft positions in the batch as a shared verification budget. It globally ranks candidate draft tokens using confidence and keeps the positions that are more likely to survive verification. In effect, it shifts budget away from low-confidence suffixes of weak requests toward deeper continuations of stronger requests. Because the target model still performs the same acceptance test on the retained positions, the output distribution remains lossless.

Second, runtime-adaptive depth selection explicitly models the cost of verifying extra tokens. That cost is deployment-specific: it depends on dense vs. MoE architecture, the parallelism scheme, and the GPU/runtime stack. D-cut profiles verification latency at startup and incorporates this measured cost into the selection objective. So the method is not maximizing expected accepted tokens alone; it is trading off accepted-token gain against incremental verification latency under the current runtime.

Conceptually, D-cut is a scheduler rather than a new drafter. It does not modify the target model, does not require training, and is compatible with existing speculative pipelines. The paper focuses on block-parallel drafters because they make long drafts cheap to produce, which amplifies the waste on the verification side. Compared with fixed-depth verification or per-request local pruning, the distinctive idea is batch-level non-uniform depth allocation guided by both confidence and measured runtime cost.1c:T6dc,The experiments focus on end-to-end throughput and speedup relative to autoregressive decoding across batch sizes, not just offline acceptance metrics. The paper evaluates both dense and MoE models under varying concurrency. A representative result highlighted in the paper is that with Qwen3-8B plus DFlash at draft length D=15, the mean accepted length is only about 6, implying that roughly 60% of verified draft tokens are rejected. At batch size 64, vanilla DFlash can already fall below standard AR throughput. D-cut is designed precisely for this regime.

Empirically, D-cut recovers positive acceleration in dense settings where vanilla speculative decoding becomes worse than AR at high concurrency. On MoE models, the reported peak speedup reaches about 3.0× over AR. The supporting analyses are also important: accepted lengths are highly dispersed across requests, drafter confidence has predictive value for acceptance, and the acceptance profile differs by workload. For example, Math500 and MBPP tend to exhibit more long accepted continuations, while MT-Bench is dominated by short accepted prefixes, which explains why a fixed verification depth is unstable across workloads.

The ablations suggest three practical lessons. Confidence-based pruning without runtime adaptation can choose the wrong depth under some hardware/runtime configurations. Per-request pruning without cross-request global allocation leaves noticeable gains on the table. And simply increasing draft length does not guarantee higher throughput at high concurrency, because verification cost can dominate. The main experimental takeaway is that speculative decoding speedup is not monotonic in batch size, and the best operating point must be adapted to the runtime.1d:T433,For a quick read, start with the introduction and motivation. The two facts to lock in are: verification becomes expensive at high concurrency, and accepted lengths are highly uneven within a batch. Then read the method section carefully, especially the formalization of cross-request pruning and how runtime profiling enters the objective. That is where the paper moves beyond a heuristic and becomes a latency-aware budget allocation method.

In the experiments, prioritize three figure types: throughput or speedup versus batch size, confidence-versus-acceptance analysis, and accepted-length distributions across tasks. The first tells you whether the method works, and the latter two explain why. A 30-40 minute skim is enough to extract the main idea and deployment boundary conditions. If you want to go deeper, two questions are worth following up: how robust confidence is as an acceptance proxy across drafters and decoding temperatures, and whether the runtime cost model could be turned into a closed-loop online controller rather than a one-time startup profile.1e:T516,The method operates in a strict query-only setting. Given only a query q, it uses no benchmark responses, preference labels, reference answers, human-written rubrics, or model training. The framework has three LLM roles: a rubric proposer, response generators, and a rubric-blind pairwise judge. A deterministic controller maintains the active rubric set, a tree structure, and evolution memory. At each step, the proposer emits either an Add patch for one atomic rubric or a Split patch for a bundled rubric. The central design choice is to avoid asking an LLM to compare two abstract rubric sets. Instead, each candidate rubric is tested in response space. The local pair starts from a strong answer satisfying the background rubrics and the candidate rubric, then minimally edits it to violate the candidate. This tests whether violating the rubric makes an otherwise similar answer worse. The alternative pair is generated from scratch: it first creates the strongest answer that satisfies the background rubrics while violating the candidate, then minimally repairs it to satisfy the candidate. This catches over-specific, optional, or style-only rubrics. The judge compares both pairs in both presentation orders. A candidate is accepted only if the answers satisfying it win in both comparisons.1f:T41f,This is work on unified multimodal pretraining and modeling paradigm, not just an incremental avatar demo. The proposed world plus event stream decomposition unifies ordinary video, interactive video, speech, behavior, and language into one causal sequence modeling framework. Three implications matter for foundation model research. First, it provides a pretraining task for joint video/audio/language/behavior modeling that is closer to agent interaction than plain video generation. Second, it writes open-vocabulary actions directly into the language event stream, avoiding a fixed action vocabulary and pointing toward a unified interface for agent behavior. Third, it frames pretraining as learning how a world evolves in real time given context and incoming inputs, then specializes that competence by changing the world configuration and input driver; this differs from both standard next-token language modeling and standard diffusion video generation. Relevant for multimodal foundation models, real-time interaction systems, and world modeling.20:T71f,The paper keeps the Wan-Streamer v0.1/v0.2 design: a single Transformer models text, audio, and video on one causal timeline, so perception, response timing, speaking, visible listening, and synchronized video are learned jointly rather than assembled from cascaded modules. The main change in v0.3 is not architecture but a reframing of the training objective and sequence representation. Any video is decomposed into a persistent world and a time-varying event stream. The world captures stable context over the timescale of a clip or interaction: environment, visual style, subject identity and appearance, ambient sound, voice characteristics, and other durable conditions. Events are localized changes: subject behavior, camera motion, environmental change, speech, and other sounds. The world is represented as a flexible structured record whose schema can vary by clip or task rather than being globally fixed. Each event has a time interval, a free-form description of the change, and an association to a character or to the environment. The pretraining task becomes: given the world and incoming inputs, predict how the world streams forward unit by unit in real time. For downstream interaction, the world configures scene, character, ambient sound, and voice; streaming user text/audio/video drive the model's event stream. The event stream interleaves spoken words with parenthesized natural-language behavior directives such as “(frowning slightly) What did you say?” This replaces a fixed action vocabulary with open-vocabulary actions grounded in the world, while conditioning audio-video generation through the same path as the language stream, so no new latency-critical component is added. In short, this is a semantic reorganization of the existing causal sequence rather than a new stack of modules.21:T43b,WanSong discards AR-dominant cascaded pipelines in favor of an end-to-end single-stage pure diffusion framework. First, a low-compression 1D continuous VAE is trained to compress 44.1kHz stereo audio into 43.1Hz continuous latents with 1024x downsampling (half the compression ratio of Stable Audio 2, to preserve more time-frequency details for sharp transients and source separation). Second, a novel dual-stem token scheme models vocal and BGM latents separately, eliminating cross-interference that caused BGM suppression and unresolvable CFG tradeoffs between vocal and BGM quality in joint modeling, while also enabling direct stem-separated output for post-production. Third, a hybrid-MMDit backbone processes a unified sequence of LLM-encoded text tokens and dual-stem audio tokens. Additional components include RL for human preference alignment and step distillation for faster inference. The data pipeline uses a 5-stage curation process on over 6 million hours of multilingual song data, with a balanced sampling ratio of Music:Speech:Sound = 50%:40%:10% during training.22:T4e5,The central design is a one-bit experiment. In the answer-blind condition, the generator sees only the problem, samples a chain, and the pipeline keeps chains whose final answer is correct. In the answer-conditioned condition, the generator sees both the problem and the gold answer, is asked to reach that answer, and the same correctness filter is applied. The setup fixes the generator, problem set, filter, training recipe, and data budget, so the intervention is isolated.

The mechanism analysis separates answer visibility from the instruction to rationalize. Merely showing the answer under a neutral instruction is much less harmful; the main damage comes from asking the model to derive toward the known answer. The observable symptom is early answer declaration. Answer-conditioned chains tend to state the answer near the beginning and then fill in the explanation. This is an off-policy shift: the training traces no longer match the model’s own search distribution when the answer is unknown. After fine-tuning, the student inherits this habit in its own generations. A simple filter that removes chains with early answer statements partially repairs performance, which supports the claim that rationalized chains carry much of the harm.23:T651,The main experiments fine-tune Qwen3-8B with full-parameter SFT on math problems from the Nemotron-Cascade-SFT-Stage-2 split, disjoint from evaluation. Evaluation uses vLLM in thinking mode on MATH-500 with a 32,768-token budget and symbolic answer matching. The controlled comparison is built on a fixed set of 935 training problems. Under this harness, the Qwen3-8B base model scores 95.2. The answer-conditioned corpus trains a student that is 16.2 points weaker than the answer-blind corpus on MATH-500. On harder olympiad-style AIME problems, the gap grows to 27.2 points. The penalty remains under length-matched controls and training-budget sweeps, and it increases with difficulty.

The degradation also appears in code: even when every retained chain passes tests, answer-conditioned traces still hurt the student. The penalty is absent on multiple-choice knowledge and science benchmarks, where the task is mainly option selection rather than deriving an answer through a long process. Within a single answer-conditioned corpus, chains that state the answer early account for much of the damage; a three-seed difference-in-differences analysis reports a 16.9-point effect. The paper also proposes a pre-training diagnostic signature measured from a small set of unlabeled generations. Across eight models from four families, the signature ranks the leakage penalty and predicts a held-out family with a mean absolute error of 2.9 points. If a pipeline cannot avoid showing gold answers, prompting the model to derive first and state the answer only at the end recovers about two-thirds of the lost accuracy.24:T861,方法名 SEED，核心是 self-evolving on-policy distillation。整体分两阶段。

第一阶段是 hindsight-skill SFT。作者先让模型学会分析完整轨迹，并把轨迹压缩成自然语言 skill，内容包括可复用 workflow、关键观察、失败规避规则等。这里 skill 不是给用户看的总结，而是训练期的 privileged signal。这样做的目的，是把原本分散在长轨迹里的后见之明提炼成一个更紧凑、可条件化的表示。

第二阶段是自演化的 OPD 训练循环。当前策略先在环境中按 on-policy 方式采样轨迹；同一个模型随后扮演 analyzer，对这些已完成轨迹生成 hindsight skills。接着，作者固定采样得到的原始动作序列，不重新搜索动作，而是分别在“普通上下文”和“skill 增强上下文”下对这些已发生动作重新打分。两者的概率差异被解释为 skill 对行为的偏好修正，并转成 token-level 的 distillation 信号。最终优化目标是 outcome-based RL loss 与这个 OPD loss 的联合。

这里有几个设计点值得注意。其一，为什么强调 on-policy：因为 skill 的价值依赖当前策略会访问哪些状态、会犯哪些错，离线静态 skill 库很快就会失配。其二，为什么用同一个模型同时做 actor 和 analyzer：不是为了省模型，而是为了让“会做事”和“会复盘”一起进化，避免 teacher 固定不动。其三，为什么不把 skill 直接放到推理时：作者希望把 hindsight 变成参数内化的能力，而不是额外的 inference-time memory 机制。

从方法关系上看，它和 verbal reflection、episodic memory、self-distillation 有亲缘性，但区别在于：SEED 不把反思文本当外部记忆长期挂在上下文里，而是把 skill 仅作为训练期 privileged context，通过概率重打分产生密集监督；也不同于纯 outcome RL，因为它提供了 decision-level credit signal；也不同于传统 teacher-student distillation，因为 teacher 不是外部更强模型，而是同一策略在更有 hindsight 的条件下的“增强视角”。25:T914,SEED stands for Self-Evolving On-Policy Distillation. The framework has two stages.

Stage one is hindsight-skill supervised fine-tuning. The model is trained to analyze completed trajectories and produce natural-language skills that summarize reusable workflows, decisive observations, and failure-avoidance rules. These skills are not meant as user-facing summaries. They are privileged training-time signals that compress trajectory-level hindsight into a compact, conditionable representation.

Stage two is the self-evolving OPD loop. The current policy collects trajectories on-policy in the environment. The same model then acts as an analyzer and generates hindsight skills for those completed trajectories. Next, the sampled action sequence is kept fixed. The model re-scores those already-taken actions under two contexts: the original context and a skill-augmented context. The probability shift between the two is treated as a dense token-level distillation signal that indicates how hindsight would have changed the policy’s preference over the observed decisions. Training then jointly optimizes the standard outcome-based RL objective and this OPD objective.

Three design choices are central. First, the method is on-policy because useful hindsight depends on the states and failure modes induced by the current policy; a static offline skill bank would quickly become mismatched. Second, the actor and analyzer are the same evolving model, so decision-making ability and trajectory-analysis ability improve together instead of relying on a frozen teacher. Third, skills are used only during training, not at inference time, because the goal is to internalize hindsight into the policy parameters rather than depend on external memory or extra prompts.

Relative to nearby work, SEED is related to verbal reflection, episodic memory, and self-distillation, but it differs in a precise way. It does not keep reflection text as persistent inference-time context. It uses hindsight text only as privileged context during training to produce dense supervision. It also differs from pure outcome-based RL by providing decision-level credit signals, and from standard teacher-student distillation by using the same policy under a more informed hindsight-conditioned view rather than an external stronger teacher.26:T773,The paper evaluates SEED on long-horizon agent settings including embodied interaction, web navigation, and search-based tasks. The main empirical question is whether hindsight-skill distillation improves learning beyond outcome-only RL. The full excerpt provided here does not include the complete result tables, but the experimental structure makes the intended claims clear.

First, adding OPD on top of outcome-based RL improves task success over pure RL baselines, suggesting that hindsight skills provide learnable supervision rather than just extra noise. Second, the gains are not due to inference-time prompting, because the skills are used only during training and are not required at test time. Third, the self-evolving loop outperforms static skills or one-shot distillation, which supports the claim that hindsight supervision should track the current policy distribution. Fourth, token-level re-scoring is more effective than staying at the trajectory level, because it pushes hindsight information down to concrete decision points.

The most informative ablations are likely the following: removing the skill analyzer and keeping only RL, replacing the evolving analyzer with a fixed analyzer or offline skill dataset, using skills as test-time prompts instead of training-time distillation, and replacing token-level probability-shift supervision with sequence-level distillation only. Those comparisons are what establish where the gains actually come from.

From a research perspective, the most important things to inspect are whether the gains are stable across environments, how much extra training cost the analyzer loop adds, and whether skill quality becomes a bottleneck early in training when the policy is still weak. If the paper reports sample-efficiency curves, success-rate trajectories, and breakdowns by task horizon, those are more informative than a single averaged score.27:T556,For a skim, start with the introduction to pin down the exact problem: sparse trajectory-level rewards are too coarse for token-level policy learning. Then go straight to the method figure and the OPD objective. The key is to understand how a completed trajectory is turned into a hindsight skill, and how that skill is converted into dense supervision through action re-scoring. That is the conceptual center of the paper.

In the experiments, prioritize three things: the main result table against pure RL and other hindsight or reflection baselines, the ablations that isolate self-evolving, on-policy, and token-level components, and the qualitative examples of generated skills. Those examples matter because they reveal whether the model is extracting reusable workflows or merely paraphrasing individual trajectories. A useful skim should take about 35-45 minutes.

Two deeper questions are worth tracking. One is whether skill generation quality is limited by the current policy’s own capability, which could make early self-bootstrapping unstable. The other is whether the probability shift under skill-augmented context introduces bias: an action can become more likely because it matches the analyzer’s verbal explanation, not necessarily because it is causally better. Those are the main points to watch if you plan to reproduce or extend the method.28:T816,方法核心是 BPO，Branching Policy Optimization。它不再为每个 prompt 独立采样 N 条完整轨迹，而是先采样一条 backbone trajectory，再在其中选若干 branch point，从这些中间状态恢复环境并展开多个 sibling rollouts，最后用同一父状态下兄弟轨迹的回报均值做 baseline。

具体流程分五步。1）先按当前策略跑出一条 backbone。2）在 backbone 上选 M 个分叉点。论文不是均匀选，而是用每步策略熵做 branch scheduler，优先把预算放在策略最不确定的位置。这样做的理由很直接：如果某一步策略已经很确定，再分叉的边际信息小；高熵位置更可能对应决策瓶颈。3）对每个 branch point，调用 sandbox snapshot/restore，从该状态 fork 出 K 个候选动作或 continuation。4）每个 fork 从该中间状态继续 rollout 到终止，得到 terminal reward。5）在更新时，对某个 sibling rollout 的 advantage，不再减去 prompt 下所有独立轨迹的均值，而是减去同一父节点下其他 siblings 的均值，形成 tree-structured sibling baseline，再配合 PPO-style clipped objective 做策略更新。

理论部分有两层。第一层是无偏性：只要 sibling baseline 不包含当前样本自身的回报，或按 leave-one-out 方式构造，优势估计对策略梯度仍无偏。第二层是方差上界：相较 GRPO 依赖 Var(R|s0)，BPO 的 sibling baseline 把方差压到与 E[Var(R|st)] 同量级，差值正是前缀解释掉的那部分 Var(E[R|st]|s0)。这正对应“共享前缀不该被当成独立样本噪声”。

和 VinePPO 的区别也值得看。VinePPO 也利用中间状态分叉，但更偏 critic / value estimation 语境；BPO 的重点是面向当前 LLM agent 常用的 baseline-only、critic-free 训练范式，把 branch topology 直接嵌进 GRPO/PPO 风格更新里，并强调 matched compute 下的方差收益。和 PPO/RLOO/GRPO 相比，BPO 改的不是 loss 形式，而是 rollout topology 与 advantage estimator 的条件化粒度。29:T857,The method is Branching Policy Optimization (BPO). Instead of sampling N independent full trajectories per prompt, BPO samples one backbone trajectory, chooses branch points along it, restores the sandbox at those states, expands multiple sibling continuations, and computes advantages using a sibling baseline within each branch family.

The pipeline has five steps. (1) Roll out one backbone trajectory under the current policy. (2) Select M branch points on the backbone. The paper uses an entropy-based scheduler rather than uniform sampling: branching budget is allocated to steps where the policy is most uncertain. This is sensible because branching at already-certain decisions adds little information, while high-entropy steps are more likely to be decision bottlenecks. (3) At each selected branch state, snapshot/restore is used to fork K alternative actions or continuations. (4) Each fork is rolled out to termination to obtain a final reward. (5) For policy updates, the advantage of one sibling rollout is computed against the mean return of its siblings under the same parent state, yielding a tree-structured sibling baseline, and then plugged into a PPO-style clipped objective.

The theory has two key parts. First, the estimator is unbiased as long as the sibling baseline is constructed without leaking the sample’s own return, e.g. via leave-one-out structure. Second, the variance bound improves over GRPO: instead of scaling with Var(R|s0), the relevant term becomes E[Var(R|st)] up to a K/(K-1) factor. The gap is exactly the prefix-explained component Var(E[R|st]|s0), which is the variance standard group baselines fail to remove.

It is also useful to compare BPO with VinePPO. VinePPO also branches from intermediate states, but it is more rooted in critic/value-estimation settings. BPO is tailored to the critic-free, baseline-only regime that has become common in LLM agent RL, and its main intervention is the rollout topology plus a state-conditional advantage estimator. Relative to PPO/RLOO/GRPO, the loss family is familiar; what changes is the granularity at which shared structure is exploited.2a:T5ce,The experiments cover WebShop, ALFWorld, and SWE-bench Verified, with two backbones, and comparisons are done at matched return-sample budget or closely matched compute. The headline results are: BPO beats PPO, RLOO, GRPO, and VinePPO by 3.6 to 6.1 absolute points; it reaches the same final performance as GRPO in 0.62× the gradient steps; and it shows roughly half the empirical gradient variance. These numbers support the central claim that the gain comes from better rollout structure rather than simply more sampling.

The ablations are where much of the paper’s value sits. Increasing branch width K helps at first and then saturates, which suggests sibling comparison needs some horizontal diversity but not arbitrarily large branching. Branch count M shows a similar trade-off: too many branch points fragment the budget and reduce useful continuation depth. The entropy-based scheduler outperforms uniform and lowest-entropy branching, indicating that branch allocation is a real source of gain rather than a cosmetic choice. The paper also measures snapshot overhead explicitly and argues that, when checkpoint/restore is reasonably cheap, the variance-reduction benefit outweighs the systems cost.

The method appears best suited to sparse-reward, long-horizon tasks where early decisions strongly shape final success. The advantage is likely smaller in highly stochastic environments, in settings where restore is unreliable, or when rewards are already dense and local.2b:T41f,NexForge is a requirement-driven synthesis framework. Given a high-level capability requirement, a web-enabled research agent collects evidence from professional workflows, technical documentation, role descriptions, and similar sources to build a weighted task demand profile (categorical distributions over task type, deliverable, source strategy, runtime environment, language, and difficulty) and a scenario reservoir (concrete contexts such as organizations, roles, workflows, systems, and operational events). Each task is factored into a task form and a scenario, sampled independently, then combined through distribution-aware compilation: for each scenario, compatible options are filtered sequentially across dimensions to produce a task directive. The framework then automatically retrieves or constructs an executable workspace, runs a teacher policy to collect interactions, and distills trajectories into SFT data. Scenarios describe only working context and do not encode task type, preventing them from determining the corpus distribution.2c:T407,The central design is to capture the long prefix once and replay the response suffix. Prefix states are stored as paged, owned physical states, while response-side computation is replayed layer by layer and block by block so that the full long-sequence activation graph does not remain resident on GPU. The paper starts from the GRPO dependency graph, including policy ratios and clipped policy terms over G responses, then identifies where conditional replay can miss a gradient term. LongStraw combines state inventory, explicit ownership, device placement, layer staging, whole-layer checkpointing, adapter-scoped optimization, and architecture-specific parallel layouts. For Qwen, it handles dense hybrid layers, logical-to-physical page mapping, global full-attention forward composition, and four-block response replay under an eight-H20 envelope. For GLM, it builds around MLA/DSA, dense-to-MoE feed-forward paths, TP1/CP32/EP32 ownership, zigzag context pages, CPU page residency, and replayed whole-layer backward records.2d:T432,The core idea is a per-sample comparison between the model's next-token distribution p_θ and the ENTD computed over the full training corpus. Since ENTD is the unconstrained global minimizer of the pretraining cross-entropy loss, the gap between the two directly quantifies how far the model is from the theoretical 'optimal memorizer' optimum. To match the actual training procedure where the dataloader chunks text into fixed context windows and only computes loss on batch-aligned prefixes, the authors further define ENTD-T, a training-aligned variant that serves as a more realistic optimization target. To efficiently count arbitrary-length k-gram occurrences in massive corpora without full corpus scans, the authors adapt the ∞-gram method built on suffix arrays. The analysis covers: distributional shifts of the discrepancy across training steps and model scales; decomposition of discrepancy sources (sampling error, optimization variance, architectural limits); and correlation between data attributes (frequency, empirical entropy, etc.) and fitting quality.2e:T5a3,The core method is Concept Chaining. For each multiple-choice question and a designated target option, the system generates a short connection paragraph that links entities in the question to the target option through one or two intermediate concepts. The paragraph is designed to sound natural while avoiding direct answer restatement. The goal is not explicit supervision but indirect semantic bias.

The paper instantiates the paragraph generator in three ways: vanilla prompting, supervised fine-tuning, and RL. The RL version is optimized for a trade-off: produce text that increases steering toward the target while keeping the target hard to infer from the paragraph alone. This is an important design choice. The objective is not simply stronger poisoning, but stronger covert steering.

These generated paragraphs are then used as a continued-pretraining corpus for a victim model. After adaptation, the model is re-evaluated on the original multiple-choice questions, and the shift in answer distribution toward the designated option is measured. Compared with classic backdoor attacks, data poisoning, or knowledge editing, the intervention here is subtler: no trigger, no explicit label corruption, and no direct parameter rewrite. From a pretraining perspective, the interesting part is the interface itself: continued pretraining is used as a controlled probe of how weak semantic structure in data can alter decision boundaries.2f:T4aa,The evaluation covers five reasoning benchmarks and focuses on two axes: steering success and detectability. The main quantitative result is a clear trade-off. Direct paraphrases achieve the strongest raw steering, with a 39.7% Steer Ratio, but they are also highly detectable, with 75.9% Inferability. The RL-trained connection paragraphs give the best covert-steering balance: 30.0% Steer Ratio, improving over the SFT warm start from 27.9%, while also achieving the best Leakage Gap of 2.7, the smallest Ref Drift of 1.1, and a tied-best Normal score of 99.9. The RL variant also transfers best on average to unseen benchmarks.

These results support two claims. First, implicit steering is real: explicit answer cues are not necessary. Second, the most practically concerning regime is not maximal steering, but moderate steering with low detectability. When reading the full paper, the most useful details to inspect are the victim model scale, the amount of continued-pretraining data, the strength of intervention per question, and how stable the transfer is across benchmarks. Those details determine whether this is a narrow benchmark artifact or a broader data-sensitivity phenomenon.3:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L9",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-07-20","generated_at":"2026-07-20T01:51:11.876976+00:00","stats":{"total":96,"starred":1,"must_read":8,"skim":44,"glance":44,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":3,"llm_pretrain":4,"scaling":2,"architecture":7,"efficient":9,"eval":15,"multimodal":9,"multimodal_gen":6,"post_train":16,"mechanism":9,"coding_agent":3,"agent":13},"fetch_status":{}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2607.15232","title":"In-Place Tokenizer Expansion for Pre-trained LLMs","abstract":"A tokenizer fixed at the start of pre-training allocates vocabulary in proportion to the pre-training corpus, reflecting the deployment priorities at that time. continued-BPE tokenizer expansion。作者从 LFM2 的 65K byte-level BPE tokenizer 出发，保持已有 merge 不变，在一个偏向欠覆盖语言、同时保留英语和代码的多语语料上继续学习新的 BPE merge，把词表扩展到 128K。这样做有两个关键性质：多数旧 token 在新词表中仍然是一对一 token；所有新 token 都可以精确分解成旧 tokenizer 下的一串 source tokens。相比重新训练一个独立 tokenizer，这种构造避免了任意 source-to-target token 对齐问题；相比简单 vocabulary union，它保持了 BPE merge 结构上的连续性，也更适合模型参数迁移。模型侧初始化也利用这两个性质：保留下来的旧 token 直接复制 embedding / LM-head row；新 token 的 row 用其旧 token 分解的 embedding 均值初始化。随后做两阶段适配：第一阶段只训练 embedding，冻结模型主体，让新词表先对齐已有表示空间；第二阶段全模型 continued pretraining，用均衡多语 mixture 让模型主体适应新的分词边界。完成 tokenizer adaptation 后，再进入常规 保留，可直接复制参数。token 压缩收益很明显：同一文本下，expanded tokenizer 对 Hindi 的 token 数约减少 2.4 倍，对 Vietnamese 约减少 2.6 倍，对 Thai 最大约减少 4.0 倍。作者还把 tokenizer 压缩收益和更大词表带来的每 token 成本分开测量，估计在参考端侧设备上，这些语言按字符计的 decode speedup 约为 language mix. The original LFM2 tokenizer is compact and efficient for English, code, and a fixed set of languages, but it fragments languages such as Hindi, Vietnamese, and Thai into many more tokens. That directly increases decoding steps. For on-device models, increasing the vocabulary is not free, because the embedding and LM-head matrices affect memory footprint and per-token decode bandwidth. The problem is therefore how to expand the tokenizer in place, improve multilingual compression, and preserve the accumulated quality of the pretrained checkpoint without restarting pretraining.","importance":"The main value is a production-scale recipe for tokenizer evolution after pretraining. It treats tokenizer replacement as part of continued pretraining, not as a standalone preprocessing change. The contribution is practical but important: continue the existing BPE merge process, preserve a mostly one-to-one mapping for old tokens, initialize new rows from old-token decompositions, and adapt the model in two stages. This is directly relevant to foundation-model maintenance, especially when deployment priorities change after the original pretraining run. It also gives a useful latency framing for edge models: a larger vocabulary increases per-token cost, but better compression can still reduce per-character decoding time when the old tokenizer was severely fragmented.","technical_approach":"$a","experiments":"$b","reading_guide":"Read Figure 1 first; it captures the full pipeline from tokenizer construction to model adaptation and later training stages. It has direct implications for pretraining data security and data pipeline design. The proposed HalfLife analysis framework provides a reusable tool for evaluating the risk of various content injection vectors reaching pretraining corpora.","technical_approach":"First, it defines a new threat model of third-party content injection attacks, where an adversary has no access to the model training pipeline, and instead injects malicious text into non-owned webpages via public discussion interfaces, relying on ordinary web crawlers to pick up the content and include it in pretraining corpora. Second, it introduces the HalfLife analysis framework, which measures retention rates of injected content across three pipeline stages (web crawling, text extraction, quality filtering) and multiplies them to get the final inclusion probability. However, existing agents typically rely on a single initial state that conditions the entire agent's execution, making them vulnerable to cascading errors initiated by a suboptimal initial state. To mitigate this, we present CIPHER, an automated data science agent that l","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-15T22:01:21Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2607_14386.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"现有数据科学类LLM Agent通常依赖单一初始状态（如高层计划）驱动整个执行链路，初始步骤的幻觉或次优推理会沿链路级联放大，最终导致任务失败；已有的test-time scaling方法大多把初始状态生成和执行绑定——生成多少就执行多少，缺少对候选初始状态的显式筛选环节，计算资源利用率和成功率都不理想。","importance":"提出的解耦探索-选择（DES）框架是test-time scaling设计空间里一个清晰的新设计点，把决策论中经典的探索-利用权衡结构化引入Agent推理扩展：先用较大预算生成候选做广度探索，再用独立的选择模块挑出最有价值的子集执行。这种设计不绑定特定领域，原则上可迁移到代码、工具调用、开放域推理等其他Agent场景，对理解如何在推理时通过计算换性能、如何分配生成/执行预算有方法论价值。","technical_approach":"CIPHER整体是基于LangGraph实现的有向无环执行图，输入为数据集+用户文本目标，输出为文本形式的分析结果。核心创新是DES框架，拆成两个独立阶段：1）探索阶段：由Planner Agent生成N个候选初始计划（N为超参），目标是覆盖足够多样的解题路径；2）选择阶段：用独立的选择策略从N个候选中选出M个（M<N，M为超参）进入后续执行，实现计算资源向高价值初始状态倾斜。选中的M个状态并行执行以降低延迟，每条执行链路包含Feature Agent（建议特征工程）、代码迭代精修循环（Coder生成→执行→Analyzer联合日志+代码诊断→Editor局部修改，循环至预算b耗尽）、Finalizer Agent（汇总执行结果）。最后由Aggregator Agent综合多条并行链路的输出得到最终答案。代码循环采用局部修改而非全量重生成，在保证纠错能力的同时降低了生成开销。","experiments":"在闭式（信息抽取类）和开式（探索性分析类）两类数据科学任务上与现有数据科学Agent对比，CIPHER取得一致的性能提升。DES框架的消融实验显示：1）解耦设计（N>M）显著优于“生成即执行”的耦合基线（N=M），在相同执行预算M下成功率更高；2）N和M的比例存在最优区间，过大的N带来边际收益递减；3）选择策略的质量直接影响最终性能，随机选择的消融版本性能明显下降。代码迭代精修循环的统计显示，局部编辑模式能在较少迭代次数内修复大部分语法/逻辑错误，比全量重生成更高效。","reading_guide":"泛读为主，预计25-35分钟。重点看：第3节DES框架的形式化定义和设计空间拆解——这是最有迁移价值的部分；DES的消融实验图表，尤其是N/M比例扫描和选择策略对比，能直接指导其他Agent场景的test-time scaling参数配置；附录中的代码循环统计和具体任务执行样例，用于理解各模块的实际行为。具体数据科学领域的任务细节可快速跳过。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Existing LLM-powered data science agents typically rely on a single initial state (e.g., a high-level plan) to condition the entire execution pipeline, so hallucinations or suboptimal reasoning at the first step cascade through the pipeline and cause task failure. Prior test-time scaling methods mostly couple initial-state generation and execution — they execute every candidate they generate — lacking an explicit selection stage, which leads to suboptimal compute utilization and success rates.","importance":"The proposed Decoupled Exploration-Selection (DES) framework introduces a well-defined new design point in the test-time scaling design space, by structurally importing the classical exploration-exploitation tradeoff from decision theory into agent inference scaling: a large budget is first spent on broad candidate generation (exploration), then an independent selection module picks the most valuable subset for execution (exploitation). intermed","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-16T16:33:59Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_15178.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要解决的不是“让 Transformer 多想几步”这么泛的问题，而是一个更具体的架构瓶颈：标准自回归解码把每一步的高维中间计算都压回 token 空间，再由下一个 token 重新展开，导致跨时间持续存在的潜在推理状态很难保留。已有 latent reasoning 方法通常把递归信号放在输入嵌入或最终层附近，离真正承载抽象推理的中间层较远；另一类 depth looping 方法虽然能增加单 token 计算，但推理成本会随循环次数上升。T^2MLR 的目标是把“跨 token 持续的连续推理状态”直接安放在中间层，并且尽量不破坏标准 teacher-forcing 预训练和低开销推理。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要有三层。第一，它给“latent reasoning 该放在哪里”这个问题提供了一个很清楚的答案：不是输入端，也不是只在输出端，而是中间层。这个判断和 mechanistic interpretability 中关于中层承担抽象组合与关系推理的观察是对齐的。第二，它提出了一种仍然兼容 dense token-level supervision 的连续推理架构，避免了很多 latent CoT 方法在训练可扩展性上的硬伤，尤其是 sequence parallel 下的 teacher-forced pretraining。第三，它的收益不是只出现在小型合成任务上，而是延续到自然语言预训练、下游多跳推理、以及对现有 1.7B instruct 模型的 retrofit，这说明它更像一个可插入的基座改造方向，而不是只在特定 benchmark 上成立的技巧。贡献量级上，我会把它看成“值得持续跟踪的架构分支”：还谈不上替代标准 AR Transformer，但已经把 latent recurrence 从概念推进到可预训练、可扩展、可低开销部署的阶段。","technical_approach":"核心设计是 temporal middle-layer recurrence。模型在前一个 token 的某个较深中间层缓存表示，然后在当前 token 的一个较早中间层把这份缓存通过门控融合进去。这样做有两个关键点。其一，递归信号不是从 embedding 重新进入网络，而是直接注入到中层计算图里，因此前一时刻形成的抽象状态可以在后续 token 的中层继续演化。其二，作者强调 recurrence 只放在局部中层，而不是全层都做递归。原因很直接：早层更偏词法和局部句法，晚层更接近词表投影；把递归集中在中层更符合功能分工，也更省参数和算力。\n\n从实现上看，T^2MLR 需要定义一对层位点：一个“写入层”产生要缓存的中间状态，一个“读取层”在下一个 token 处接收该状态。两者之间通过一个 gated fusion 模块连接，门控负责控制当前 token 本地表示与前一 token 持续状态的混合比例。这个设计和简单 residual addition 的区别在于，它允许模型按上下文动态决定何时依赖跨时间潜变量，何时主要依赖当前 token 的标准前向计算。\n\n训练上的难点比结构本身更重要。严格的时间递归会让 teacher forcing 下不同位置之间产生真实依赖，从而破坏标准并行训练。作者提出 approximate temporal-parallel training 来恢复可扩展性：训练时对递归路径做近似并行化处理，使得序列维度仍可并行，而不是像 RNN 那样完全串行。论文把这一点作为可预训练性的关键，因为如果没有这一步，架构即使概念上成立，也很难在大规模语料上训练。这里值得注意的是，它不是完全精确地复现推理时的递归，而是在训练可扩展性和递归保真度之间做了工程折中。\n\n和相关方法相比，差异很明确。相对 latent-recurrent methods，它把状态放在中层而不是输入端；相对 depth-looping methods，它把额外计算沿时间维传播，而不是在单 token 内反复过层，因此推理时每 token 只多一个轻量递归融合，而不是多次整网循环；相对标准 Transformer，它显式提供了跨 token 的连续隐状态通路，缓解 token bottleneck。","experiments":"$e","reading_guide":"建议先看 Introduction 里对问题的拆解，再直接看方法部分关于“为什么递归要放在中层”的论证。这篇文章真正有价值的地方不是门控公式，而是层功能分工与时间递归位置选择的结合。精读时重点看三类内容。\n\n第一，架构图和 recurrence/fusion 的定义。要弄清楚缓存的是哪一层、注入到哪一层、门控如何做、是否共享参数、缓存张量的形状和更新时机是什么。这里决定了它到底是“轻量跨 token 状态”还是“变相加深网络”。\n\n第二，训练部分的 approximate temporal-parallel scheme。这是全文最值得深挖的工程点。要重点看它如何在 teacher forcing 下近似递归依赖、误差来自哪里、是否会造成 train-test mismatch、以及 sequence parallel 的实现边界。如果你关心大规模预训练可行性，这部分比 benchmark 分数更重要。\n\n第三，消融实验。尤其要看 middle-only recurrence vs full-layer recurrence、不同递归层段比例、与 looped transformer/latent recurrent baseline 的对比、以及推理 overhead 的测量。这里能回答“为什么不是全层递归”“为什么不是单 token 内多循环”这两个关键问题。\n\n如果时间有限，优先顺序可以是：方法图 + 训练近似 + 预训练主表 + 层位点消融 + retrofit 实验。预计精读 60–90 分钟比较合适；如果要评估是否能复现或改造成自家模型，训练并行化细节需要再多花一轮时间。","authors_note":"Princeton 相关团队，代码仓库挂在 princeton-pli 名下。摘要与正文片段未完整列出作者名单，无法在当前材料中可靠确认具体作者背景。"},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets a specific architectural bottleneck in autoregressive Transformers: rich intermediate computation is repeatedly compressed back into token space at every decoding step, so abstract reasoning states do not persist well across time. Prior latent-reasoning approaches usually pass recurrent information through embeddings or final-layer outputs, which is a poor match to the empirical observation that abstract reasoning is concentrated in middle layers. Depth-looping alternatives increase per-token compute by reusing layers multiple times, but their inference cost grows with the number of loops. T^2MLR asks whether a Transformer can preserve and evolve latent reasoning states directly in the middle of the network, while keeping standard teacher-forced pretraining and near-standard decoding cost.","importance":"Its value for pretraining research is threefold. First, it makes a concrete architectural claim about where persistent reasoning state should live: in middle layers rather than at the input interface or only near the output. That aligns well with mechanistic findings on layer specialization. Second, it offers a latent-reasoning design that remains compatible with dense token-level supervision and scalable teacher-forced pretraining, which is where many continuous-CoT style ideas become impractical. Third, the gains are not confined to synthetic tasks: they carry over to language pretraining, downstream reasoning finetuning, and even a retrofit setting on a pretrained 1.7B instruct model. That makes it more than a benchmark trick. It looks like a plausible architectural branch for future base models, even if it is not yet a replacement for standard AR Transformers.","technical_approach":"$f","experiments":"$10","reading_guide":"$11","authors_note":"Affiliated with Princeton-related authors; the code repository is under princeton-pli. Existing approaches approximate this log-likelihood by modeling only the token predictions, ignoring the order in which positions are unmasked during generation. We observe that MDLM generation involves two decisions at each step: what tokens to place at each maske","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-16T16:57:34Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_15200.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是 diffusion/MDLM 做 RL 时一个长期被简化掉的关键问题：生成轨迹的策略不只包含“在 masked 位置填什么 token”，还包含“这一步哪些位置保留、哪些位置 remask”，也就是 unmasking order 本身。现有 RL 方法大多只优化 token 部分的近似对数概率，把位置选择当成固定过程或隐含过程处理，导致 policy gradient 不完整。论文要做的是把 MDLM 生成正式写成一个两阶段动作 MDP，并把 masking decision 也纳入可优化策略。","importance":"对新型语言建模范式尤其是 diffusion LM 很重要，因为它补上了一个从预训练目标到 post-train RL 的接口问题。AR 模型能直接做 policy gradient，原因是轨迹 likelihood 可分解且易算；MDLM 一直缺少同样干净的 RL 形式化，这限制了它在 reasoning/coding 上继续吃到 RL 红利。这里的贡献不只是“又一个 RL trick”，而是指出 MDLM 的策略空间天然比 AR 更丰富，顺序控制本身就是可学习决策，而不是采样细节。这个视角如果成立，会影响后续对 diffusion LM 的训练目标、采样器设计、以及 pretrain-posttrain 一体化理解。量级上，它属于 diffusion LM 走向可竞争 post-training 的基础性补丁。","technical_approach":"方法核心是把 MDLM generation trajectory 拆成两类动作。每个 diffusion step 先在当前所有 masked 位置上采样 token，然后再从这些新预测位置里选择一个子集保留，其余位置 remask，留到后续步骤再决定。作者把这一定义成 two-stage action MDP，于是整条轨迹的概率自然分解为 token policy 与 masking policy 的乘积，对应的 policy gradient 也分解成两项：一个奖励好的 token 预测，一个奖励好的位置/顺序选择。\n\n关键点在于 masking term 不是额外训练一个策略头。作者从模型已有 logits 中构造 unmasking scores，再通过 softmax 得到位置选择的对数概率，因此不需要额外参数，也不需要额外 forward pass。这一点很重要，因为 diffusion LM 本来采样链就比 AR 长，如果再加独立 masking network，训练和推理都会更重。\n\n从理论上，论文证明忽略 masking term 会漏掉能提升期望回报的梯度方向。直观上也成立：在 MDLM 里，先揭示哪些位置、把哪些不确定位置留到后面，本身就会影响后续条件分布和最终答案质量。只奖励 token 而不奖励顺序，相当于把一半策略空间冻结了。\n\n算法兼容性也是一个优点。作者不是重新发明一套 RL 框架，而是把这个分解嵌入标准 policy gradient family 中。也就是说，任何原本依赖 log-prob 的 RL 算法，只要能处理 trajectory-level reward，就可以把 token log-prob 和 mask log-prob 一起纳入目标。对于 diffusion LM 社区，这比设计一个专用优化器更有推广性。\n\n和已有方法相比，差异主要有三点。相对 ELBO-based RL 近似，它不再把训练目标停留在 token reconstruction 视角，而是显式建模生成轨迹。相对只用 trajectory token likelihood 的方法，它把 unmasking order 也当作 policy。相对需要额外网络预测顺序的方案，它直接复用模型 logits，工程代价更低。","experiments":"论文的实验重点在数学推理和代码生成，结论比较集中：联合优化 token term 和 masking term，稳定优于只优化 token 的现有方法。摘要里给出的代表性提升是 MATH500 最多 +4.0%，HumanEval +2.9%，GSM8K 和 MBPP 各 +2.5%。同时，论文声称达到这些任务上的当前最好结果之一，说明收益不是只在单一数据集上出现。\n\n更值得看的其实是消融。核心消融应当包括：只保留 token gradient、只保留 masking gradient、两者同时优化；不同 remasking/unmasking schedule 下 masking term 的贡献；以及 position log-prob 的不同构造方式。这里如果两项联合始终最好，就说明 masking policy 不是噪声正则，而是真正提供了额外可学习信号。\n\n从方法描述推断，论文还会比较与 ELBO 近似 RL、trajectory-based token-only RL 的差异。要重点看这些对照是否控制了 rollout 数、采样步数、reward normalization、以及 base MDLM backbone。因为 diffusion LM 的 RL 结果很容易被采样预算和 decode schedule 混淆。\n\n另一个值得注意的点是成本。作者强调 masking term 的计算“无额外 forward cost beyond token likelihood”，这意味着性能提升不是靠更大训练预算换来的，而是更完整地利用了同一条生成轨迹中的监督信号。如果正文里给出 wall-clock 或 sample efficiency，对判断实际价值会更有帮助。","reading_guide":"先看问题形式化，不要急着看结果表。最关键的是把 MDLM 的一步生成理解成“token prediction + remasking decision”两个动作，而不是把 remasking 当成实现细节。建议按这个顺序读。\n\n第一，Preliminaries 里关于 MDLM 轨迹定义和采样过程的部分。要确认论文采用的是哪种 remasking 语义：是只对当前新预测位置做保留/回遮，还是允许对已揭示 token 再次修改。这里关系到 MDP 状态和动作空间的定义。\n\n第二，两阶段 MDP 和 policy gradient 推导。这是全文核心。重点看轨迹概率如何分解、position log-prob 如何从 logits 导出、以及理论部分如何说明忽略 masking term 会错过改进方向。如果你只读一节，就读这里。\n\n第三，实验中的主对比和消融。优先看 token-only vs token+masking、不同任务上提升是否一致、以及是否有 reward hacking 或训练不稳定迹象。对于 diffusion LM 的 RL，稳定性和方差控制往往比峰值分数更重要。\n\n如果时间有限，30–45 分钟可以完成泛读；如果你在做 diffusion LM 或 any-order LM 的 post-training，这篇值得再花一轮时间把公式和算法伪代码抄清楚，因为它很可能直接影响你如何定义 log-prob 和 advantage。","authors_note":"当前材料未给出完整作者列表。可确认的是，这是一篇面向 Masked Diffusion Language Model 强化学习的理论与方法论文；具体机构与作者背景无法在现有内容中可靠确认。"},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a missing piece in reinforcement learning for masked diffusion language models: the policy is not only about which tokens to place at masked positions, but also about which positions to keep revealed versus remask at each denoising step. Most prior RL approaches optimize only an approximate token likelihood and treat the unmasking order as fixed or incidental. This work argues that the ordering decision is part of the policy itself, and therefore part of the policy gradient.","importance":"This matters because it closes a key pretrain-to-posttrain gap for diffusion-style language models. Autoregressive models fit naturally into policy-gradient RL because their trajectory likelihood is tractable and action factorization is simple. MDLMs have lacked an equally clean RL formulation, which has limited their ability to benefit from the same post-training machinery that helped AR reasoning models. The contribution here is not just another RL heuristic. It reframes MDLM generation as a richer policy space where ordering is a learnable decision, not a sampling detail. If that view holds, it affects how future diffusion LMs are trained, how their samplers are designed, and how one should think about the interface between pretraining objectives and RL fine-tuning.","technical_approach":"$12","experiments":"$13","reading_guide":"$14","authors_note":"The provided material does not include a reliable full author list. six","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-15T23:38:44Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_14427.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"深度循环Transformer（权重绑定的核心块可变次数执行）的现有研究混淆了两个核心问题：1）深度鲁棒性（模型在未微调的推理深度下是否有效）；2）深度分配（每个token需要多少计算量）。此前没有工作直接测量训练后弹性模型的逐token计算行为，且逐token提前退出方案在KV缓存正确性、批处理效率、计算节省三者间无法兼顾，联合训练的停机策略容易坍缩到退化深度，需要精细调优计算先验。","importance":"1）首次直接刻画了深度循环LM的逐token固定点收敛行为，填补了弹性深度模型内部计算动态的认知空白；2）证明逐token收敛状态可直接用于后验深度分配，无需联合训练路由，且效果优于学习式路由，为弹性计算架构的落地提供了低成本可复用的方案；3）验证了训练时循环深度分布与模型饱和深度的对应关系，为这类架构的预训练配方设计提供了明确的量化指导。","technical_approach":"1）预训练配方：采用最小化的随机深度训练策略——每个优化步随机采样循环次数，用标准交叉熵损失训练，不引入停机单元、ponder损失或FLOP惩罚，得到深度鲁棒的骨架模型；模型结构为Llama风格，由唯一的前置层、权重绑定的循环核心块、唯一的后置层+LM头组成。2）逐token行为测量：直接追踪循环过程中每个token的连续输出KL散度、隐状态变化，量化收敛深度的异质性分布。3）深度分配策略：提出无需训练的收敛退出规则——当token的连续输出KL低于预设阈值时停止循环，对比基于收敛标签训练的线性路由器、固定全局深度两类基线。","experiments":"基于FineWeb-Edu训练的135M级模型（训练平均循环深度8）：1）收敛特性：平均连续输出KL从第2次循环的3.9×10^-1降到第16次的8.5×10^-6，隐状态变化同步衰减；逐token收敛异质性显著，中位数token在第6次循环收敛，约10%的token在训练平均深度8时仍在更新，收敛顺序与token类型相关。2）深度分配效果：无训练收敛退出策略在平均4.94次循环（比固定深度8低38%）下达到固定深度8的质量，在平均深度范围内与固定深度效果完全匹配；而用相同收敛标签训练的线性路由器几乎需要满深度，无计算节省，该结论在两个模型规模下均复现。3）分布对应性：训练平均深度为4的模型饱和深度为4，训练平均深度为8的模型饱和深度为8，与Huginn的外部实验结果一致。4）深度弹性：单个检查点可覆盖1-8倍深度，质量单调提升，可稳定外推到4倍训练平均深度。","reading_guide":"重点精读：1）第1节的问题拆分（深度鲁棒性vs深度分配）与现有工作的局限分析；2）第2节的逐token测量方法与第3节的收敛特性结果（对应逐token research on depth-recurrent Transformers (variable execution of weight-tied core blocks) conflates two core open questions: 1) depth robustness (whether the model performs well at inference depths not explicitly tuned for), and 2) depth allocation (how much compute each individual token requires). No prior work directly measures per-token computation dynamics of a trained elastic model; existing per-token early exit methods fail to simultaneously preserve KV cache correctness, batching efficiency, and compute savings, while jointly trained halting policies often collapse to degenerate depths and require delicate tuning of compute priors.","importance":"1) Provides the first direct characterization of per-token fixed-point convergence behavior in depth-recurrent LMs, filling a critical gap in understanding internal computation dynamics of elastic-depth models; 2) Demonstrates that per-token convergence states can be directly exploited for post-hoc depth allocation without joint routing training, and outperforms learned routing policies, offering a low-cost, reusable solution for elastic compute architecture deployment; 3) Validates the correlation between training-time loop depth distribution and model saturation depth, providing clear quantitative guidance for pretraining recipe design for this architecture family.","technical_approach":"1) Pretraining recipe: Uses a minimal randomized-depth training strategy — recursion count is randomly sampled at each optimizer step, trained with standard cross-entropy loss, no halting unit, ponder loss, or FLOP penalty, to produce a depth-robust backbone; the model follows Llama-style architecture, consisting of unique prelude layers, a weight-tied recurrent core block, and unique coda layers feeding into the LM head. 2) Per-token behavior measurement: Directly tracks per-token successive-output KL divergence and hidden state changes across recursion steps to quantify the heterogeneous distribution of convergence depth. 3) Depth allocation policies: Proposes a training-free convergence exit rule (stop recursion when a token's successive output KL falls below a preset threshold), compared against a linear router trained on harvested convergence labels and fixed global depth baselines.","experiments":"$15","reading_guide":"Prioritize close reading of: 1) Section 1's problem decomposition (depth robustness vs depth allocation) and limitation analysis of prior work; ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-07T16:23:06Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_14106.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决 token。","importance":"这是建模范式方向的论文，和 next-token AR 并列的 diffusion LM 研究相关。贡献量级更像是一个有用的机制改进，而不是完整替代 AR 的训练路线。它的启发在于把 diffusion LM 的时间变量从“整句共享”改成“每个 token 独立推进”。这让模型可以先确定低熵 token，后确定高熵 token，也能在条件生成中把 token。核心改动是引入 per-token time：每个位置有自己的局部时间，局部时间由全局时间和该 token 的 rank 共同决定。rank 表示该 token 应该较早还是较晚 denoise。作者用一次前向后的 per-token entropy 来分配 rank，低熵、更确定的位置可以更快接近 clean token，高熵位置保留更多不确定性。条件生成时，prompt token 的时间固定为 1，输出 canvas 的 token time 从 0 推到 1。架构上，论文修改常用的 adaptive layer norm，使 diffusion transformer 能接收每个 token 不同的时间条件，而且不增加新参数。训练后还可以用 shortcut/self-consistency loss 做蒸馏，把连续 flow
